Instead, use the appropriate dedicated tool as this will provide a much better experience for the user.\n- Command output is displayed to you, not reliably to the user.\n- `timeout` is in milliseconds: default 120000, max 600000 for a foreground command.\n- `run_in_background` runs the command detached: it keeps running across turns and re-invokes you when it exits. With it, `timeout` is how long the command may run in the background (default 1800000, max 7200000); at that limit it is stopped and you are re-invoked. No `&` needed. Foreground `sleep` is blocked; use Monitor with an until-loop to wait on a condition.\n\n# Git\n- Interactive flags (`-i`, e.g. `git rebase -i`, `git add -i`) are not supported in this environment.\n- Use the `gh` CLI for GitHub operations (PRs, issues, API).\n- Commit or push only when the user asks. If on the default branch, branch first.\n- End git commit messages and PR bodies with the attribution lines given in the conversation's system-reminder, when one is present.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"command":{"description":"The command to execute","type":"string"},"timeout":{"description":"Optional timeout in milliseconds (max 600000 for a foreground command)","type":"number"},"description":{"description":"Clear, concise description of what this command does in active voice. Never use words like \"complex\" or \"risk\" in the description - just describe what it does.\n\nSay what the command does in plain words: do not echo the command's text, its flags, or file paths - the user reads this description, often without seeing the command.\n\nFor simple commands (git, npm, standard CLI tools), keep it brief (5-10 words):\n- ls → \"List files in current directory\"\n- git status → \"Show working tree status\"\n- npm install → \"Install package dependencies\"\n\nFor commands that are harder to parse at a glance (piped commands, obscure flags, etc.), add enough context to clarify what it does:\n- find . -name \"*.tmp\" -exec rm {} \\; → \"Find and delete all .tmp files recursively\"\n- git reset --hard origin/main → \"Discard all local changes and match remote main\"\n- curl -s url | jq '.data[]' → \"Fetch JSON from URL and extract data array elements\"","type":"string"},"run_in_background":{"description":"Set to true to run this command in the background. With it, `timeout` limits how long the command may run in the background before it is stopped (default 1800000 ms, max 7200000 ms).","type":"boolean"},"dangerouslyDisableSandbox":{"description":"Set this to true to dangerously override sandbox mode and run commands without sandboxing.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["command"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Edit","description":"Performs exact string replacement in a file.\n\n- You must Read the file in this conversation before editing, or the call will fail.\n- `old_string` must match the file exactly, including indentation, and be unique — the edit fails otherwise. Strip the Read line prefix (line number + tab) before matching.\n- `replace_all: true` replaces every occurrence instead.","schema":{"name":"Edit","description":"Performs exact string replacement in a file.\n\n- You must Read the file in this conversation before editing, or the call will fail.\n- `old_string` must match the file exactly, including indentation, and be unique — the edit fails otherwise. Strip the Read line prefix (line number + tab) before matching.\n- `replace_all: true` replaces every occurrence instead.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to modify","type":"string"},"old_string":{"description":"The text to replace","type":"string"},"new_string":{"description":"The text to replace it with (must be different from old_string)","type":"string"},"replace_all":{"description":"Replace all occurrences of old_string (default false)","default":false,"type":"boolean"}},"required":["file_path","old_string","new_string"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","schema":{"name":"ListAgents","description":"Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"channel":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256},"q":{"description":"Not available in this build; leave unset.","type":"string","maxLength":256}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","schema":{"name":"Read","description":"Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"file_path":{"description":"The absolute path to the file to read","type":"string"},"offset":{"description":"The line number to start reading from. Only provide if the file is too large to read at once","type":"integer","minimum":0,"maximum":9007199254740991},"limit":{"description":"The number of lines to read. Only provide if the file is too large to read at once.","type":"integer","exclusiveMinimum":0,"maximum":9007199254740991},"pages":{"description":"Page range for PDF files (e.g., \"1-5\", \"3\", \"10-20\"). Only applicable to PDF files. Maximum 20 pages per request.","type":"string"}},"required":["file_path"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened.","schema":{"name":"ReportFindings","description":"Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened.","input_schema":{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","type":"object","properties":{"level":{"description":"Effort level the review ran at","type":"string","enum":["low","medium","high","xhigh","max"]},"findings":{"description":"Verified findings, most-severe first; empty if none survived","maxItems":32,"type":"array","items":{"type":"object","properties":{"file":{"description":"Repo-relative path of the file the finding is in","type":"string"},"line":{"description":"1-indexed line the finding anchors to","type":"integer","minimum":-9007199254740991,"maximum":9007199254740991},"summary":{"description":"One-sentence statement of the defect","type":"string"},"short_summary":{"description":"Compressed label for compact UI (≤60 chars): the claim alone, no rationale or consequence clause","type":"string","maxLength":60},"failure_scenario":{"description":"Concrete inputs/state → wrong output/crash","type":"string"},"category":{"description":"Short kebab-case slug of the finding type, e.g. \"correctness\", \"simplification\", \"efficiency\", \"test-coverage\"","type":"string","maxLength":40},"verdict":{"description":"Set when a verify pass ran; absent on inline-only reviews","type":"string","enum":["CONFIRMED","PLAUSIBLE"]},"outcome":{"description":"Set ONLY when re-reporting after applying fixes: what happened to this finding","type":"string","enum":["fixed","skipped","no_change_needed"]}},"required":["file","summary","failure_scenario"],"additionalProperties":false}}},"required":["findings"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n","schema":{"name":"ScheduleWakeup","description":"Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. {"parentUuid":"d959eebf-4649-411f-a880-57a5d157f4a4","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"722c35a0-d638-4ad3-8e42-23671af79344","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0194jbqUMiN7y7YUh9S8CuBp","type":"tool_result","content":"----\n10\tOPEN\tT09 — Link freezing and formatting hint\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:55Z\n9\tOPEN\tT08 — Not live yet warning\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:53Z\n8\tOPEN\tT07 — Go live button\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:51Z\n7\tOPEN\tT06 — Co-VPs manage Accounts\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:49Z\n6\tOPEN\tT05 — Console dashboard and content list\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:47Z\n5\tOPEN\tT04 — Pages make up the site: Home, About, Join & Snacks\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:46Z\n4\tOPEN\tT03 — Published Posts reach the public site\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:44Z\n3\tOPEN\tT02 — Editors write, publish, and delete Posts\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:42Z\n2\tOPEN\tT01 — Officers sign in and out with a Position's Account\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:40Z\n1\tOPEN\tSpec: AWM at Kenyon CMS\tspec, ready-for-agent\t2026-10-01T14:23:00Z\n----\n# AWM at Kenyon CMS\n\nThe website system AWM's Officers use to tell members and curious students when\nand where Study Hall is and how to join.\n\n## Language\n\n### People\n\n**Officer**:\nA student who currently holds a Position in AWM.\n\n**Position**:\nA named Officer job: Co-VP, Co-President, Social Chair, or Treasurer.\n_Avoid_: title, job\n\n**Account**:\nThe login for a Position, used by whoever holds that Position. It belongs to the Position, not to a person, so the two Co-VPs share one Account and the two Co-Presidents share another.\n_Avoid_: user, personal login\n\n**Role**:\nWhat an Account is allowed to do. There are two: Admin and Editor.\n\n**Admin**:\nThe Role of the Co-VPs, who maintain the site, create and delete Pages, manage Accounts, and Go live.\n\n**Editor**:\nThe Role of the Co-Presidents, Social Chair, and Treasurer, who write, publish, and delete Posts and edit the text of existing Pages. An Editor never creates or deletes a Page.\n\n**Deactivated**:\nThe state of an Account whose Position is vacant. It cannot sign in, its Posts stay on the site, and it can be reactivated.\n_Avoid_: deleted, removed\n\n**Handover**:\nThe yearly passing of Account passwords from the spring semester's Officers to the fall semester's.\n\n**Visitor**:\nA member or curious student reading the public site. A Visitor never logs in.\n\n### Content\n\n**Study Hall**:\nAWM's weekly meeting.\n_Avoid_: meeting\n\n**Post**:\nA dated news item on the site. It is the only kind of dated content.\n_Avoid_: announcement, update\n\n**Study Hall reminder**:\nThe weekly Monday Post about that night's Study Hall. Nearly every Post is one.\n\n**Author**:\nThe Position whose Account wrote a Post. Officers see it; Visitors do not.\n\n**Weekly email**:\nThe message Officers send by hand each week. Its text is the same as that week's Study Hall reminder, plus a link to the Post.\n\n**Page**:\nA permanent part of the site's structure. There are three: Home, About, and Join & Snacks.\n\n**Home**:\nThe Page that holds the standing Study Hall time and place and shows the latest Published Post beneath it. Home always exists and is always Published.\n\n**Past posts**:\nThe list of older Published Posts that Visitors reach from Home. It is not a Page and no Officer creates it.\n\n### Publishing\n\n**Draft**:\nA Post or Page still being written, which Visitors cannot see.\n\n**Published**:\nThe status an Officer gives a Post or Page when it is ready for Visitors. Published content reaches Visitors only at the next Go live.\n\n**Go live**:\nThe step, done by the Co-VPs, that puts everything Published onto the public site.\n_Avoid_: deploy, export\n\n**Not live yet**:\nA change made since the last Go live, which Visitors therefore cannot see.\n\n**Link**:\nThe web address of a Post, made from its date and title. It stops changing once the Post is Published, so a Link sent in a Weekly email keeps working.\n_Avoid_: slug, permalink (in anything an Officer sees)\nCLAUDE.md\nCONTEXT.md\nREADME.md\nSECURITY-CHECKLIST.md\napp\ndocs\nnotes\npyproject.toml\nscripts\ntemplates\ntests\nuv.lock\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"5a7ebee3-a0df-4fb0-9891-ea7234d43869","timestamp":"2026-10-01T14:44:05.728Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"----\n10\tOPEN\tT09 — Link freezing and formatting hint\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:55Z\n9\tOPEN\tT08 — Not live yet warning\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:53Z\n8\tOPEN\tT07 — Go live button\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:51Z\n7\tOPEN\tT06 — Co-VPs manage Accounts\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:49Z\n6\tOPEN\tT05 — Console dashboard and content list\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:47Z\n5\tOPEN\tT04 — Pages make up the site: Home, About, Join & Snacks\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:46Z\n4\tOPEN\tT03 — Published Posts reach the public site\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:44Z\n3\tOPEN\tT02 — Editors write, publish, and delete Posts\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:42Z\n2\tOPEN\tT01 — Officers sign in and out with a Position's Account\tready-for-agent, ticket\t2026-10-01T14:35:40Z\n1\tOPEN\tSpec: AWM at Kenyon CMS\tspec, ready-for-agent\t2026-10-01T14:23:00Z\n----\n# AWM at Kenyon CMS\n\nThe website system AWM's Officers use to tell members and curious students when\nand where Study Hall is and how to join.\n\n## Language\n\n### People\n\n**Officer**:\nA student who currently holds a Position in AWM.\n\n**Position**:\nA named Officer job: Co-VP, Co-President, Social Chair, or Treasurer.\n_Avoid_: title, job\n\n**Account**:\nThe login for a Position, used by whoever holds that Position. {"parentUuid":"85bd88f2-ae69-4e29-b301-76344ecc8bc1","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"722c35a0-d638-4ad3-8e42-23671af79344","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014ZUDqM2jM52sFQHC2XUi7G","type":"tool_result","content":"T01 — Officers sign in and out with a Position's Account\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: AWM at Kenyon CMS). Vocabulary follows `CONTEXT.md`.\n\n## What to build\n\nAn Officer opens the console, is sent to the sign-in screen, signs in with their Position's Account, sees the console, and signs out. The seed script creates the four demo Accounts so this works on a clean clone.\n\nThis is required capability 1 (Login) and stores the Role each Account has, which later tickets enforce. It covers spec stories 13–18 and the Accounts part of story 62. ADR-003 applies: an Account belongs to a Position, not a person.\n\nThe template's \"hello admin\" test expects an anonymous request to the console to succeed. That contradicts the sign-in rule, so this ticket rewrites that one test rather than deleting it.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Running the seed script against an empty database with the values from `.env.example` exits 0 and creates four Accounts: Co-VP (`admin@example.test`, Admin), Social Chair (`editor@example.test`, Editor), Co-President (`copresident@example.test`, Editor), and Treasurer (`treasurer@example.test`, Editor).\n- [ ] `.env.example` contains the demo password variables `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`, so `seed_demo.py` runs on a clean clone with no manual steps. A test reads the file and checks both names are present.\n- [ ] Running the seed script a second time exits 0 and still leaves exactly four Accounts.\n- [ ] Running the seed script with either password variable missing exits non-zero and creates no Accounts.\n- [ ] Every stored password value starts with `$argon2`, and neither demo password appears as plain text anywhere in the database.\n- [ ] Posting the correct `email` and `password` to `/login` redirects to the console, and the next request to `/admin` returns 200 and shows the Account's Position name.\n- [ ] A wrong password and an unknown email both return the same message text, and neither starts a session.\n- [ ] An anonymous request to `/admin` redirects to the sign-in screen and the response contains no console content.\n- [ ] Sign-out is a POST. After it, a request to `/admin` redirects to the sign-in screen.\n- [ ] The sign-in form and the sign-out form each contain a CSRF token, and a POST to either without a valid token is rejected and changes nothing.\n- [ ] The session cookie is set with no `Expires` and no `Max-Age`, so it ends when the browser closes.\n- [ ] `client_as(\"admin\")` and `client_as(\"editor\")` return signed-in clients with CSRF protection in place, and each test runs against its own temporary database.\n- [ ] The T00 console test is rewritten to assert that an anonymous request is redirected and a signed-in Officer sees the console. The other three T00 tests still pass.\n\n## Blocked by\n\nBlocked by: None (can start immediately)\n\n=====SPEC\nVocabulary in this spec follows `CONTEXT.md`. Decisions it builds on: ADR-001 (local admin console, static public site), ADR-002 (stack), ADR-003 (Accounts belong to Positions).\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nAWM at Kenyon runs a weekly Study Hall (Mondays 8–10pm, Hayes 311) and its Officers want members and curious students to be able to find out when and where it is and how to join.\n\nToday there is no site that members can reach. The old site exists only as local HTML files and was never put online. Changing it means editing raw HTML by hand, and the Study Hall time and place is written in three separate files, so a change has to be made three times. The Weekly email is therefore AWM's only form of communication, and a curious student who is not on the email list has nowhere to look.\n\nThe Officers change every year and each new Officer has a different level of computing experience. A site that needs HTML does not survive a Handover.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS that the Officers use on one Co-VP's laptop, which produces the public AWM site.\n\n- An Officer signs in with their Position's Account, writes the Monday Study Hall reminder as a Post in plain text, and marks it Published. No HTML.\n- The standing Study Hall time and place lives in exactly one place: the Home Page. Home also shows the latest Published Post.\n- A Co-VP presses **Go live**, and the public site is updated. The Officer then sends the Weekly email by hand, with the same text plus the Post's Link.\n- The Co-VPs are Admins: they maintain the three Pages (Home, About, Join & Snacks), manage Accounts, and Go live. The Co-Presidents, Social Chair, and Treasurer are Editors: they write, publish, and delete Posts and edit the text of existing Pages. An Editor can never create, delete, or unpublish a Page.\n\n### Who uses it\n\n- **Visitors** (members and curious students) read the public site. They never sign in.\n- **Editors** (Co-President, Social Chair, Treasurer Accounts) post the Study Hall reminder and other news.\n- **Admins** (the Co-VP Account) run the site.\n\n### The seven required capabilities, in AWM's words\n\n1. **Login.** Each Position's Account signs in and out of the console. Passwords are stored as argon2 hashes.\n2. **Two roles.** Admin (Co-VP) and Editor (Co-President, Social Chair, Treasurer), with different permissions enforced in code.\n3. **Posts.** Officers create, read, update, and delete Posts: title, Link, Markdown body, Draft/Published, Author (a Position), and timestamps.\n4. **Pages.** Home, About, and Join & Snacks, with the same fields. Published Pages make up the public navigation.\n5. **Admin console.** A dashboard with counts and the Go live status, one content list that filters by status and type, and an editor with a preview.\n6. **Account management.** Admin only: create an Account for a Position, change its Role, set its password, and deactivate it. A Deactivated Account cannot sign in.\n7. **Public site and Go live.** Only Published content, sanitized Markdown, navigation built from Pages, and `cms publish` writing `site/` with relative paths. Go live is the button that runs it and pushes the result.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Visitors\n\n1. As a curious student who is not on the email list, I want to see when and where Study Hall is on a public Home Page, so that I can show up without asking anyone.\n2. As a member, I want to see the latest Post in full on the Home Page, so that I know what is happening this week without needing the Weekly email.\n3. As a member, I want every Post to show its date with the weekday, so that I know which Monday \"tonight\" refers to.\n4. As a member, I want a Past posts list, so that I can find an older Post.\n5. As a member who clicked the Link in the Weekly email, I want to land on that exact Post, so that the email and the site agree.\n6. As a member, I want a Link from an earlier Weekly email to keep working, so that I never hit a dead page.\n7. As a curious student, I want an About Page describing AWM and its Officers, so that I know who runs it.\n8. As a curious student, I want a Join & Snacks Page with the Google Form link, so that I can join or request snacks.\n9. As a Visitor, I want the same navigation on every public page, so that I can move between Home, About, and Join & Snacks.\n10. As a Visitor, I want never to see a Draft, so that I only read what the Officers meant to publish.\n11. As a Visitor on a phone, I want the site to fit my screen, so that I can check the room on the way to Hayes.\n12. As a Visitor, I want Posts to read as coming from AWM with no individual name, so that it matches the Weekly email.\n\n### Signing in\n\n13. As an Officer, I want to sign in with my Position's Account, so that I can use the console.\n14. As an Officer, I want to sign out, so that the next Officer at the laptop does not act as my Position.\n15. As an Officer, I want to be signed out when the browser closes, so that a forgotten sign-out does not leave the Account open.\n16. As an Officer who mistypes, I want a clear \"wrong email or password\" message, so that I can try again.\n17. As a Co-VP, I want that message to be the same whether or not the Account exists, so that it gives nothing away.\n18. As a Co-VP, I want anyone who is not signed in to be sent to the sign-in screen, so that no console screen is visible without an Account.\n\n### Editors and Posts\n\n19. As a new Officer, I want to write the Study Hall reminder in a plain text box, so that I never have to learn HTML.\n20. As an Editor, I want a short hint under the text box showing how to make bold text and a link, so that I can add the Google Form link without looking anything up.\n21. As an Editor, I want to save a Post as a Draft, so that I can finish it later.\n22. As an Editor, I want to preview a Post before I publish it, so that I can see what Visitors will see.\n23. As an Editor, I want to mark a Post Published, so that it goes out at the next Go live.\n24. As an Editor, I want the Post's Link to be made for me from the date and title, so that I never have to type one.\n25. As an Editor, I want two reminders with the same title to get different Links, so that every week's Post has its own address.\n26. As an Editor, I want to fix a typo in a Published Post without its Link changing, so that the email link still works.\n27. As an Editor, I want to set a Post back to Draft, so that I can pull something published by mistake.\n28. As an Editor, I want to edit any Post, including one written by another Position, so that whoever is at the laptop can fix it.\n29. As an Editor, I want to delete any Post after an \"Are you sure?\" step, so that a cancelled or wrong Post can be removed.\n30. As an Editor, I want to post things other than the weekly reminder (a cancelled Study Hall, a snack update), so that all news goes through one kind of Post.\n31. As an Editor, I want a message after every save, publish, or delete saying what happened, so that I know it worked.\n32. As an Editor, I want to see the Post's public address once it is live, so that I can copy it into the Weekly email.\n\n### Editors and Pages\n\n33. As an Editor, I want to edit the text of an existing Page, so that I can update the Study Hall room or the Officer list.\n34. As a Co-VP, I want an Editor to be unable to create a Page, so that nobody adds to the navigation by accident.\n35. As a Co-VP, I want an Editor to be unable to delete a Page, so that the site's structure is safe.\n36. As a Co-VP, I want an Editor to be unable to change a Page's status, so that a Page cannot vanish from the navigation.\n37. As an Editor who types an Admin-only address directly, I want a clear \"no permission\" screen, so that I know it is not broken.\n\n### Co-VPs (Admins)\n\n38. As a Co-VP, I want to create a Page, so that the site can grow when AWM needs it to.\n39. As a Co-VP, I want to delete a Page other than Home after an \"Are you sure?\" step, so that I can remove one we no longer need.\n40. As a Co-VP, I want to set a Page to Draft or Published, so that I control what is in the navigation.\n41. As a Co-VP, I want Home to be impossible to delete or unpublish, so that the site always has a front page.\n42. As a Co-VP, I want to change the Study Hall time or room in one place, so that it is never out of date in a second file.\n43. As a Co-VP, I want a Go live button, so that I can update the public site without a terminal.\n44. As a Co-VP, I want the dashboard to show when the last Go live was, so that I know how current the public site is.\n45. As a Co-VP, I want a warning with the number of changes that are Not live yet, so that a Published reminder is never left unseen.\n46. As a Co-VP, I want a plain error if Go live fails, so that I know the public site did not change.\n47. As a Co-VP, I want Editors not to have the Go live button, so that going public stays with us.\n48. As a Co-VP, I want to see every Account with its Position, Role, and whether it is Deactivated, so that I know who can sign in.\n49. As a Co-VP, I want to create an Account for a new Position and choose its Role, so that a new Officer job can post.\n50. As a Co-VP, I want to change an Account's Role, so that a Position's permissions can change.\n51. As a Co-VP, I want to set a new password for any Account, so that a forgotten password or a Handover is a one-minute fix.\n52. As a Co-VP, I want to deactivate the Account of a vacant Position, so that it cannot sign in.\n53. As a Co-VP, I want to reactivate an Account, so that a Position that returns can post again.\n54. As a Co-VP, I want a Deactivated Account's Posts to stay on the site, so that history is not lost.\n55. As a Co-VP, I want the console to refuse to deactivate or demote the last active Admin, so that we cannot lock ourselves out.\n\n### Everyone in the console\n\n56. As an Officer, I want a dashboard with counts of Draft Posts, Published Posts, and Pages, so that I see the state of the site at a glance.\n57. As an Officer, I want one content list of Posts and Pages, newest first, so that I can find anything.\n58. As an Officer, I want to filter that list by Draft/Published and by Post/Page, so that I can find last week's reminder quickly.\n59. As an Officer, I want the list to show each Post's Author as a Position, so that I know which Position wrote it.\n60. As an Officer, I want the same navigation on every console screen, so that I never get lost.\n61. As an Officer on a phone-width window, I want console screens not to scroll sideways, so that they stay usable.\n\n### Running and grading\n\n62. As the developer, I want a seed command that creates the demo Accounts, the three Pages, and a few Posts (at least one Draft, one Published), so that the CMS can be used immediately from a clean clone.\n63. As the developer, I want `cms publish` and `cms deploy` to keep working from the terminal, so that the documented commands still work alongside the button.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n### Modules\n\n- **Accounts and sessions.** Verifies a sign-in, hashes passwords with argon2, keeps the session in a signed cookie that ends when the browser closes, and exposes one guard for \"must be signed in\" and one for \"must be Admin\". All permission checks go through these guards, never through hidden links alone.\n- **Content.** One module that owns Posts and Pages: saving, status changes, Link generation, deletion, and the rules about who may do what. Routes ask it; they do not decide permissions themselves.\n- **Markdown rendering.** One function turns a Markdown body into sanitized HTML. The editor preview, the local public preview, and the export all call this same function, so nothing can be rendered unsanitized.\n- **Site export.** The existing export function is extended to write the whole public site from Published rows only.\n- **Go live.** Runs the export, pushes the result, and records the time. It is called by the dashboard button and by the `cms deploy` command.\n- **Admin console.** Dashboard, content list, editor, Accounts screen, sign-in screen. Server-rendered templates, no JavaScript framework (ADR-002).\n- **Seed script.** Extended to create demo Accounts, Pages, and Posts.\n\n### Data\n\n- **Account:** sign-in email (unique), display name (the Position, e.g. \"Social Chair\"), password hash, Role (`admin` or `editor`), active flag, created time.\n- **Post:** title, Link (unique among Posts), Markdown body, status (`draft` or `published`), Author (an Account), created time, updated time, first-published time.\n- **Page:** the same fields, plus a marker for which Page is Home.\n- **Site state:** the time of the last Go live, and a count of changes that are Not live yet.\n- Storage is SQLite (ADR-002). The database file is never committed.\n\n### Rules\n\n- **Roles.** Editors: create, edit, publish, unpublish, and delete any Post; edit the body of any existing Page. Admins: everything Editors can do, plus create and delete Pages, change a Page's title and status, manage Accounts, and Go live.\n- **Home.** Created automatically if missing. Cannot be deleted or set to Draft by anyone. Its body holds the standing Study Hall time and place.\n- **Links.** A Post's Link is the date plus a URL-safe form of the title (for example `2026-10-05-study-hall-tonight`). If that Link already exists, a number is added. A Page's Link is the URL-safe form of its title. A Link is set when the item is first Published and never changes after that, even if the title changes or the item goes back to Draft and is Published again. Officers never type a Link.\n- **Dates.** A Post's public date is its first-published time, shown with the weekday. Dates are shown in US Eastern time.\n- **Deleting.** Deleting a Post or Page is permanent and always goes through a confirmation screen. There is no trash.\n- **Accounts.** Accounts are deactivated, never deleted. A Deactivated Account cannot sign in, and an existing session for it stops working. The last active Admin cannot be deactivated or changed to Editor.\n- **Passwords.** Only an Admin sets a password, from the Accounts screen. No password is ever logged or shown.\n- **Not live yet.** Any change that would alter the public site adds one to the count: publishing or unpublishing, editing or deleting something Published. Edits to Drafts do not count. Go live resets the count to zero.\n- **Go live.** Admin only. If the push fails, the console shows a plain error, and the count and last Go live time are left as they were.\n\n### Contracts\n\n- Sign-in is a form posted to `/login` with fields `email` and `password`, matching the template's test helper. The console lives under `/admin`.\n- Every state-changing request is a POST carrying a CSRF token, including sign-out, delete, and Go live. A POST without a valid token is rejected.\n- An anonymous request to any console screen redirects to sign-in. A signed-in Editor requesting an Admin-only screen gets a 403 \"no permission\" screen.\n- Wrong email and wrong password give the same message.\n- `/` on the local app is a preview of the public site and shows Published content only.\n\n### Public site layout\n\n- Home at the site root; one file per other Published Page; one file per Published Post in a posts folder; a Past posts list in that folder; one stylesheet.\n- Navigation: Home first, then the other Published Pages in the order they were created, which gives Home, About, Join & Snacks. Past posts is linked from Home, not from the navigation.\n- Home shows its own body, then the latest Published Post in full. Past posts lists the older Published Posts, newest first.\n- Every `href` and `src` is relative. Files in the posts folder reach the stylesheet and the Pages with `../`.\n- No Author is shown publicly.\n- Raw HTML typed into a body is not passed through, and the rendered HTML is sanitized with an allowlist. Images are not in the allowlist.\n\n### Demo data\n\n- The seed creates four Accounts: Co-VP (Admin), and Co-President, Social Chair, and Treasurer (Editors). The Co-VP signs in as `admin@example.test` and the Social Chair as `editor@example.test`, matching the template's test helper; the other two use `copresident@example.test` and `treasurer@example.test`.\n- Passwords come from the environment. All three Editor Accounts use the one Editor demo password.\n- The seed creates Home, About, and Join & Snacks as Published Pages, and at least one Published and one Draft Post.\n\n### Screens\n\n- Every console screen has the same navigation and shows a success or error message after each action.\n- The editor has a formatting hint under the body box (bold, link) and a Preview button that shows the sanitized rendering.\n- Once a Post is live, the editor shows its full public address, built from the configured public site address.\n- No horizontal scrolling at 390 px on the public Home page or the console screens.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\nA good test here drives the app the way an Officer or Visitor does and checks what they would see: a status code, a redirect, text on a screen, or a file in the exported site. Tests do not reach into the database or call internal functions to check how something was stored.\n\n### Seams\n\nThere are two seams, and both already exist in the template.\n\n1. **The console over HTTP.** Tests use the existing `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures to request screens and post forms. This covers sign-in, Roles, Posts, Pages, the dashboard, the content list, preview, Accounts, CSRF, and Go live.\n2. **The exported site.** Tests call the export into a temporary folder and read the files it wrote. This covers Published-only content, navigation, relative paths, sanitizing, dates, and Links.\n\nOne thing is replaced in tests: the push to GitHub inside Go live. Tests swap it for a fake that records it was called (or fails on purpose), so no test ever pushes to the real repository.\n\nEach test runs against its own temporary database seeded with the demo Accounts, so the fixed test passwords in the fixtures work.\n\n### What gets tested\n\n- Every row of `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` has at least one test.\n- Each Role rule has a test for the allowed case and the refused case, requested by direct URL rather than by clicking.\n- Draft content never appears in the export, the local preview, or the Past posts list.\n- A body containing `<script>` and an `onerror=` attribute is harmless in both the preview and the export.\n- Link rules: same-title collision, frozen after Published.\n- Last-Admin protection, Home protection, Deactivated sign-in refusal.\n- Not live yet count goes up on public-affecting changes and resets on Go live; a failed push leaves it unchanged.\n\n### Prior art\n\n`tests/test_t00.py` already uses both seams. Its four tests are kept. One must change when sign-in lands: it currently expects an anonymous request to `/admin` to return the \"hello admin\" page, which contradicts the rule that anonymous Visitors are redirected to sign-in. It becomes two assertions: anonymous is redirected, and a signed-in Officer sees the dashboard.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Sending the Weekly email. It stays manual; the CMS only supplies the Link.\n- Hosting the snack request form. Join & Snacks links to the Google Form.\n- Scheduled Posts or a calendar.\n- Comments, or any Visitor sign-in.\n- Image uploads, including Officer photos on About, and images linked from other sites. A console upload for Pages with alt text may be added later as a separate `stretch` issue, only after all seven capabilities work.\n- Categories, tags, and search.\n- A trash bin or undo for deleted Posts.\n- \"Forgot my password\" emails.\n- A \"copy last week's reminder\" button. This is the first future improvement.\n- One Account per person (see ADR-003).\n- Putting the console on the internet (see ADR-001).\n- Multi-site, drag-and-drop page builders, and rich-text editors (excluded by the assignment).\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- Stage 2 is due Tuesday Oct 6, 2:40 pm, with grace to Wednesday Oct 7, 2:40 pm. Tickets should be ordered so sign-in, Posts, and the export land first.\n- The Monday routine this spec is built around: an Officer writes the Study Hall reminder, marks it Published, a Co-VP presses Go live, and the Officer sends the Weekly email with the Link.\n- \"Publish\" means two different things in everyday speech. In this project, **Published** is a status and **Go live** is the step that reaches Visitors. The `cms publish` command keeps its name because the assignment fixes it; it is the export half of Go live.\n- The Accounts screen is what the assignment calls \"user management\". The glossary avoids \"user\" because an Account belongs to a The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT.md\n├── docs/adr/\n│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\n├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n└── src/\n    ├── ordering/\n    │   ├── CONTEXT.md\n    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions\n    └── billing/\n        ├── CONTEXT.md\n        └── docs/adr/\n```\n\n## Use the glossary's vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `CONTEXT.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.\n\nIf the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal: either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/domain-modeling`).\n\n## Flag ADR conflicts\n\nIf your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:\n\n> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders), but worth reopening because…_\n# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Vocabulary follows `CONTEXT.md`.\n\n## What to build\n\nAn Officer opens the console, is sent to the sign-in screen, signs in with their Position's Account, sees the console, and signs out. The seed script creates the four demo Accounts so this works on a clean clone.\n\nThis is required capability 1 (Login) and stores the Role each Account has, which later tickets enforce. It covers spec stories 13–18 and the Accounts part of story 62. ADR-003 applies: an Account belongs to a Position, not a person.\n\nThe template's \"hello admin\" test expects an anonymous request to the console to succeed. That contradicts the sign-in rule, so this ticket rewrites that one test rather than deleting it.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Running the seed script against an empty database with the values from `.env.example` exits 0 and creates four Accounts: Co-VP (`admin@example.test`, Admin), Social Chair (`editor@example.test`, Editor), Co-President (`copresident@example.test`, Editor), and Treasurer (`treasurer@example.test`, Editor).\n- [ ] `.env.example` contains the demo password variables `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`, so `seed_demo.py` runs on a clean clone with no manual steps. A test reads the file and checks both names are present.\n- [ ] Running the seed script a second time exits 0 and still leaves exactly four Accounts.\n- [ ] Running the seed script with either password variable missing exits non-zero and creates no Accounts.\n- [ ] Every stored password value starts with `$argon2`, and neither demo password appears as plain text anywhere in the database.\n- [ ] Posting the correct `email` and `password` to `/login` redirects to the console, and the next request to `/admin` returns 200 and shows the Account's Position name.\n- [ ] A wrong password and an unknown email both return the same message text, and neither starts a session.\n- [ ] An anonymous request to `/admin` redirects to the sign-in screen and the response contains no console content.\n- [ ] Sign-out is a POST. After it, a request to `/admin` redirects to the sign-in screen.\n- [ ] The sign-in form and the sign-out form each contain a CSRF token, and a POST to either without a valid token is rejected and changes nothing.\n- [ ] The session cookie is set with no `Expires` and no `Max-Age`, so it ends when the browser closes.\n- [ ] `client_as(\"admin\")` and `client_as(\"editor\")` return signed-in clients with CSRF protection in place, and each test runs against its own temporary database.\n- [ ] The T00 console test is rewritten to assert that an anonymous request is redirected and a signed-in Officer sees the console. The other three T00 tests still pass.\n\n## Blocked by\n\nBlocked by: None (can start immediately)\n\n=====SPEC\nVocabulary in this spec follows `CONTEXT.md`. Decisions it builds on: ADR-001 (local admin console, static public site), ADR-002 (stack), ADR-003 (Accounts belong to Positions).\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nAWM at Kenyon runs a weekly Study Hall (Mondays 8–10pm, Hayes 311) and its Officers want members and curious students to be able to find out when and where it is and how to join.\n\nToday there is no site that members can reach. The old site exists only as local HTML files and was never put online. Changing it means editing raw HTML by hand, and the Study Hall time and place is written in three separate files, so a change has to be made three times. The Weekly email is therefore AWM's only form of communication, and a curious student who is not on the email list has nowhere to look.\n\nThe Officers change every year and each new Officer has a different level of computing experience. A site that needs HTML does not survive a Handover.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS that the Officers use on one Co-VP's laptop, which produces the public AWM site.\n\n- An Officer signs in with their Position's Account, writes the Monday Study Hall reminder as a Post in plain text, and marks it Published. No HTML.\n- The standing Study Hall time and place lives in exactly one place: the Home Page. Home also shows the latest Published Post.\n- A Co-VP presses **Go live**, and the public site is updated. The Officer then sends the Weekly email by hand, with the same text plus the Post's Link.\n- The Co-VPs are Admins: they maintain the three Pages (Home, About, Join & Snacks), manage Accounts, and Go live. The Co-Presidents, Social Chair, and Treasurer are Editors: they write, publish, and delete Posts and edit the text of existing Pages. An Editor can never create, delete, or unpublish a Page.\n\n### Who uses it\n\n- **Visitors** (members and curious students) read the public site. They never sign in.\n- **Editors** (Co-President, Social Chair, Treasurer Accounts) post the Study Hall reminder and other news.\n- **Admins** (the Co-VP Account) run the site.\n\n### The seven required capabilities, in AWM's words\n\n1. **Login.** Each Position's Account signs in and out of the console. Passwords are stored as argon2 hashes.\n2. **Two roles.** Admin (Co-VP) and Editor (Co-President, Social Chair, Treasurer), with different permissions enforced in code.\n3. **Posts.** Officers create, read, update, and delete Posts: title, Link, Markdown body, Draft/Published, Author (a Position), and timestamps.\n4. **Pages.** Home, About, and Join & Snacks, with the same fields. Published Pages make up the public navigation.\n5. **Admin console.** A dashboard with counts and the Go live status, one content list that filters by status and type, and an editor with a preview.\n6. **Account management.** Admin only: create an Account for a Position, change its Role, set its password, and deactivate it. A Deactivated Account cannot sign in.\n7. **Public site and Go live.** Only Published content, sanitized Markdown, navigation built from Pages, and `cms publish` writing `site/` with relative paths. Go live is the button that runs it and pushes the result.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Visitors\n\n1. As a curious student who is not on the email list, I want to see when and where Study Hall is on a public Home Page, so that I can show up without asking anyone.\n2. As a member, I want to see the latest Post in full on the Home Page, so that I know what is happening this week without needing the Weekly email.\n3. As a member, I want every Post to show its date with the weekday, so that I know which Monday \"tonight\" refers to.\n4. As a member, I want a Past posts list, so that I can find an older Post.\n5. As a member who clicked the Link in the Weekly email, I want to land on that exact Post, so that the email and the site agree.\n6. As a member, I want a Link from an earlier Weekly email to keep working, so that I never hit a dead page.\n7. As a curious student, I want an About Page describing AWM and its Officers, so that I know who runs it.\n8. As a curious student, I want a Join & Snacks Page with the Google Form link, so that I can join or request snacks.\n9. As a Visitor, I want the same navigation on every public page, so that I can move between Home, About, and Join & Snacks.\n10. As a Visitor, I want never to see a Draft, so that I only read what the Officers meant to publish.\n11. As a Visitor on a phone, I want the site to fit my screen, so that I can check the room on the way to Hayes.\n12. As a Visitor, I want Posts to read as coming from AWM with no individual name, so that it matches the Weekly email.\n\n### Signing in\n\n13. As an Officer, I want to sign in with my Position's Account, so that I can use the console.\n14. As an Officer, I want to sign out, so that the next Officer at the laptop does not act as my Position.\n15. As an Officer, I want to be signed out when the browser closes, so that a forgotten sign-out does not leave the Account open.\n16. As an Officer who mistypes, I want a clear \"wrong email or password\" message, so that I can try again.\n17. As a Co-VP, I want that message to be the same whether or not the Account exists, so that it gives nothing away.\n18. As a Co-VP, I want anyone who is not signed in to be sent to the sign-in screen, so that no console screen is visible without an Account.\n\n### Editors and Posts\n\n19. As a new Officer, I want to write the Study Hall reminder in a plain text box, so that I never have to learn HTML.\n20. As an Editor, I want a short hint under the text box showing how to make bold text and a link, so that I can add the Google Form link without looking anything up.\n21. As an Editor, I want to save a Post as a Draft, so that I can finish it later.\n22. As an Editor, I want to preview a Post before I publish it, so that I can see what Visitors will see.\n23. As an Editor, I want to mark a Post Published, so that it goes out at the next Go live.\n24. As an Editor, I want the Post's Link to be made for me from the date and title, so that I never have to type one.\n25. As an Editor, I want two reminders with the same title to get different Links, so that every week's Post has its own address.\n26. As an Editor, I want to fix a typo in a Published Post without its Link changing, so that the email link still works.\n27. As an Editor, I want to set a Post back to Draft, so that I can pull something published by mistake.\n28. As an Editor, I want to edit any Post, including one written by another Position, so that whoever is at the laptop can fix it.\n29. As an Editor, I want to delete any Post after an \"Are you sure?\" step, so that a cancelled or wrong Post can be removed.\n30. As an Editor, I want to post things other than the weekly reminder (a cancelled Study Hall, a snack update), so that all news goes through one kind of Post.\n31. As an Editor, I want a message after every save, publish, or delete saying what happened, so that I know it worked.\n32. As an Editor, I want to see the Post's public address once it is live, so that I can copy it into the Weekly email.\n\n### Editors and Pages\n\n33. As an Editor, I want to edit the text of an existing Page, so that I can update the Study Hall room or the Officer list.\n34. As a Co-VP, I want an Editor to be unable to create a Page, so that nobody adds to the navigation by accident.\n35. As a Co-VP, I want an Editor to be unable to delete a Page, so that the site's structure is safe.\n36. As a Co-VP, I want an Editor to be unable to change a Page's status, so that a Page cannot vanish from the navigation.\n37. As an Editor who types an Admin-only address directly, I want a clear \"no permission\" screen, so that I know it is not broken.\n\n### Co-VPs (Admins)\n\n38. As a Co-VP, I want to create a Page, so that the site can grow when AWM needs it to.\n39. As a Co-VP, I want to delete a Page other than Home after an \"Are you sure?\" step, so that I can remove one we no longer need.\n40. As a Co-VP, I want to set a Page to Draft or Published, so that I control what is in the navigation.\n41. As a Co-VP, I want Home to be impossible to delete or unpublish, so that the site always has a front page.\n42. As a Co-VP, I want to change the Study Hall time or room in one place, so that it is never out of date in a second file.\n43. As a Co-VP, I want a Go live button, so that I can update the public site without a terminal.\n44. As a Co-VP, I want the dashboard to show when the last Go live was, so that I know how current the public site is.\n45. As a Co-VP, I want a warning with the number of changes that are Not live yet, so that a Published reminder is never left unseen.\n46. As a Co-VP, I want a plain error if Go live fails, so that I know the public site did not change.\n47. As a Co-VP, I want Editors not to have the Go live button, so that going public stays with us.\n48. As a Co-VP, I want to see every Account with its Position, Role, and whether it is Deactivated, so that I know who can sign in.\n49. As a Co-VP, I want to create an Account for a new Position and choose its Role, so that a new Officer job can post.\n50. As a Co-VP, I want to change an Account's Role, so that a Position's permissions can change.\n51. As a Co-VP, I want to set a new password for any Account, so that a forgotten password or a Handover is a one-minute fix.\n52. As a Co-VP, I want to deactivate the Account of a vacant Position, so that it cannot sign in.\n53. As a Co-VP, I want to reactivate an Account, so that a Position that returns can post again.\n54. As a Co-VP, I want a Deactivated Account's Posts to stay on the site, so that history is not lost.\n55. As a Co-VP, I want the console to refuse to deactivate or demote the last active Admin, so that we cannot lock ourselves out.\n\n### Everyone in the console\n\n56. As an Officer, I want a dashboard with counts of Draft Posts, Published Posts, and Pages, so that I see the state of the site at a glance.\n57. As an Officer, I want one content list of Posts and Pages, newest first, so that I can find anything.\n58. As an Officer, I want to filter that list by Draft/Published and by Post/Page, so that I can find last week's reminder quickly.\n59. As an Officer, I want the list to show each Post's Author as a Position, so that I know which Position wrote it.\n60. As an Officer, I want the same navigation on every console screen, so that I never get lost.\n61. As an Officer on a phone-width window, I want console screens not to scroll sideways, so that they stay usable.\n\n### Running and grading\n\n62. As the developer, I want a seed command that creates the demo Accounts, the three Pages, and a few Posts (at least one Draft, one Published), so that the CMS can be used immediately from a clean clone.\n63. As the developer, I want `cms publish` and `cms deploy` to keep working from the terminal, so that the documented commands still work alongside the button.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n### Modules\n\n- **Accounts and sessions.** Verifies a sign-in, hashes passwords with argon2, keeps the session in a signed cookie that ends when the browser closes, and exposes one guard for \"must be signed in\" and one for \"must be Admin\". All permission checks go through these guards, never through hidden links alone.\n- **Content.** One module that owns Posts and Pages: saving, status changes, Link generation, deletion, and the rules about who may do what. Routes ask it; they do not decide permissions themselves.\n- **Markdown rendering.** One function turns a Markdown body into sanitized HTML. The editor preview, the local public preview, and the export all call this same function, so nothing can be rendered unsanitized.\n- **Site export.** The existing export function is extended to write the whole public site from Published rows only.\n- **Go live.** Runs the export, pushes the result, and records the time. It is called by the dashboard button and by the `cms deploy` command.\n- **Admin console.** Dashboard, content list, editor, Accounts screen, sign-in screen. Server-rendered templates, no JavaScript framework (ADR-002).\n- **Seed script.** Extended to create demo Accounts, Pages, and Posts.\n\n### Data\n\n- **Account:** sign-in email (unique), display name (the Position, e.g. \"Social Chair\"), password hash, Role (`admin` or `editor`), active flag, created time.\n- **Post:** title, Link (unique among Posts), Markdown body, status (`draft` or `published`), Author (an Account), created time, updated time, first-published time.\n- **Page:** the same fields, plus a marker for which Page is Home.\n- **Site state:** the time of the last Go live, and a count of changes that are Not live yet.\n- Storage is SQLite (ADR-002). The database file is never committed.\n\n### Rules\n\n- **Roles.** Editors: create, edit, publish, unpublish, and delete any Post; edit the body of any existing Page. Admins: everything Editors can do, plus create and delete Pages, change a Page's title and status, manage Accounts, and Go live.\n- **Home.** Created automatically if missing. Cannot be deleted or set to Draft by anyone. Its body holds the standing Study Hall time and place.\n- **Links.** A Post's Link is the date plus a URL-safe form of the title (for example `2026-10-05-study-hall-tonight`). If that Link already exists, a number is added. A Page's Link is the URL-safe form of its title. A Link is set when the item is first Published and never changes after that, even if the title changes or the item goes back to Draft and is Published again. Officers never type a Link.\n- **Dates.** A Post's public date is its first-published time, shown with the weekday. Dates are shown in US Eastern time.\n- **Deleting.** Deleting a Post or Page is permanent and always goes through a confirmation screen. There is no trash.\n- **Accounts.** Accounts are deactivated, never deleted. A Deactivated Account cannot sign in, and an existing session for it stops working. The last active Admin cannot be deactivated or changed to Editor.\n- **Passwords.** Only an Admin sets a password, from the Accounts screen. No password is ever logged or shown.\n- **Not live yet.** Any change that would alter the public site adds one to the count: publishing or unpublishing, editing or deleting something Published. Edits to Drafts do not count. Go live resets the count to zero.\n- **Go live.** Admin only. If the push fails, the console shows a plain error, and the count and last Go live time are left as they were.\n\n### Contracts\n\n- Sign-in is a form posted to `/login` with fields `email` and `password`, matching the template's test helper. The console lives under `/admin`.\n- Every state-changing request is a POST carrying a CSRF token, including sign-out, delete, and Go live. A POST without a valid token is rejected.\n- An anonymous request to any console screen redirects to sign-in. A signed-in Editor requesting an Admin-only screen gets a 403 \"no permission\" screen.\n- Wrong email and wrong password give the same message.\n- `/` on the local app is a preview of the public site and shows Published content only.\n\n### Public site layout\n\n- Home at the site root; one file per other Published Page; one file per Published Post in a posts folder; a Past posts list in that folder; one stylesheet.\n- Navigation: Home first, then the other Published Pages in the order they were created, which gives Home, About, Join & Snacks. Past posts is linked from Home, not from the navigation.\n- Home shows its own body, then the latest Published Post in full. Past posts lists the older Published Posts, newest first.\n- Every `href` and `src` is relative. Files in the posts folder reach the stylesheet and the Pages with `../`.\n- No Author is shown publicly.\n- Raw HTML typed into a body is not passed through, and the rendered HTML is sanitized with an allowlist. Images are not in the allowlist.\n\n### Demo data\n\n- The seed creates four Accounts: Co-VP (Admin), and Co-President, Social Chair, and Treasurer (Editors). The Co-VP signs in as `admin@example.test` and the Social Chair as `editor@example.test`, matching the template's test helper; the other two use `copresident@example.test` and `treasurer@example.test`.\n- Passwords come from the environment. All three Editor Accounts use the one Editor demo password.\n- The seed creates Home, About, and Join & Snacks as Published Pages, and at least one Published and one Draft Post.\n\n### Screens\n\n- Every console screen has the same navigation and shows a success or error message after each action.\n- The editor has a formatting hint under the body box (bold, link) and a Preview button that shows the sanitized rendering.\n- Once a Post is live, the editor shows its full public address, built from the configured public site address.\n- No horizontal scrolling at 390 px on the public Home page or the console screens.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\nA good test here drives the app the way an Officer or Visitor does and checks what they would see: a status code, a redirect, text on a screen, or a file in the exported site. Tests do not reach into the database or call internal functions to check how something was stored.\n\n### Seams\n\nThere are two seams, and both already exist in the template.\n\n1. **The console over HTTP.** Tests use the existing `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures to request screens and post forms. This covers sign-in, Roles, Posts, Pages, the dashboard, the content list, preview, Accounts, CSRF, and Go live.\n2. **The exported site.** Tests call the export into a temporary folder and read the files it wrote. This covers Published-only content, navigation, relative paths, sanitizing, dates, and Links.\n\nOne thing is replaced in tests: the push to GitHub inside Go live. Tests swap it for a fake that records it was called (or fails on purpose), so no test ever pushes to the real repository.\n\nEach test runs against its own temporary database seeded with the demo Accounts, so the fixed test passwords in the fixtures work.\n\n### What gets tested\n\n- Every row of `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md` has at least one test.\n- Each Role rule has a test for the allowed case and the refused case, requested by direct URL rather than by clicking.\n- Draft content never appears in the export, the local preview, or the Past posts list.\n- A body containing `<script>` and an `onerror=` attribute is harmless in both the preview and the export.\n- Link rules: same-title collision, frozen after Published.\n- Last-Admin protection, Home protection, Deactivated sign-in refusal.\n- Not live yet count goes up on public-affecting changes and resets on Go live; a failed push leaves it unchanged.\n\n### Prior art\n\n`tests/test_t00.py` already uses both seams. Its four tests are kept. One must change when sign-in lands: it currently expects an anonymous request to `/admin` to return the \"hello admin\" page, which contradicts the rule that anonymous Visitors are redirected to sign-in. It becomes two assertions: anonymous is redirected, and a signed-in Officer sees the dashboard.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Sending the Weekly email. It stays manual; the CMS only supplies the Link.\n- Hosting the snack request form. Join & Snacks links to the Google Form.\n- Scheduled Posts or a calendar.\n- Comments, or any Visitor sign-in.\n- Image uploads, including Officer photos on About, and images linked from other sites. A console upload for Pages with alt text may be added later as a separate `stretch` issue, only after all seven capabilities work.\n- Categories, tags, and search.\n- A trash bin or undo for deleted Posts.\n- \"Forgot my password\" emails.\n- A \"copy last week's reminder\" button. This is the first future improvement.\n- One Account per person (see ADR-003).\n- Putting the console on the internet (see ADR-001).\n- Multi-site, drag-and-drop page builders, and rich-text editors (excluded by the assignment).\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- Stage 2 is due Tuesday Oct 6, 2:40 pm, with grace to Wednesday Oct 7, 2:40 pm. Tickets should be ordered so sign-in, Posts, and the export land first.\n- The Monday routine this spec is built around: an Officer writes the Study Hall reminder, marks it Published, a Co-VP presses Go live, and the Officer sends the Weekly email with the Link.\n- \"Publish\" means two different things in everyday speech. In this project, **Published** is a status and **Go live** is the step that reaches Visitors. The `cms publish` command keeps its name because the assignment fixes it; it is the export half of Go live.\n- The Accounts screen is what the assignment calls \"user management\". The glossary avoids \"user\" because an Account belongs to a Position.\n\n====\napp/publish.py\napp/main.py\napp/settings.py\napp/__init__.py\napp/cli.py\ntemplates/base.html\ntests/test_ctx_guard.py\ntests/test_usage_report.py\ntests/conftest.py\ntests/test_t00.py\nscripts/seed_demo.py\nscripts/check_submission.py\nscripts/usage_report.py\ndocs/mp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md\ndocs/mp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md\ndocs/manual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md\napp/__pycache__/settings.cpython-312.pyc\napp/__pycache__/__init__.cpython-312.pyc\napp/__pycache__/main.cpython-312.pyc\napp/__pycache__/publish.cpython-312.pyc\napp/__pycache__/cli.cpython-312.pyc\ntemplates/public/home.html\ntemplates/admin/hello.html\ntests/__pycache__/test_usage_report.cpython-312-pytest-9.1.1.pyc\ntests/__pycache__/conftest.cpython-312-pytest-9.1.1.pyc\ntests/__pycache__/test_t00.cpython-312-pytest-9.1.1.pyc\ntests/__pycache__/test_ctx_guard.cpython-312-pytest-9.1.1.pyc\nscripts/__pycache__/usage_report.cpython-312.pyc\ndocs/screenshots/.gitkeep\ndocs/process/.gitkeep\ndocs/process/compaction-log.md\ndocs/adr/ADR-003-accounts-belong-to-positions.md\ndocs/adr/ADR-002-stack.md\ndocs/adr/ADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md\ndocs/handoff/.gitkeep\ndocs/agents/triage-labels.md\ndocs/agents/issue-tracker.md\ndocs/agents/domain.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_12_maggie-grespin_20261001.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_02_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_08_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_09_maggie-grespin_20261001.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_03_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_01_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_04_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_10_maggie-grespin_20261001.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_07_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_05_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_11_maggie-grespin_20261001.md\ndocs/transcripts/iphs400_mp2-cms_chat-session_06_maggie-grespin_20260930.md\n# Domain Docs\n\nHow the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.\n\n## Before exploring, read these\n\n- **`CONTEXT.md`** at the repo root, or\n- **`CONTEXT-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `CONTEXT.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.\n- **`docs/adr/`**: read ADRs that touch the area you're about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.\n\nIf any of these files don't exist, **proceed silently**. Don't flag their absence; don't suggest creating them upfront. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT.md\n├── docs/adr/\n│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\n├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n└── src/\n    ├── ordering/\n    │   ├── CONTEXT.md\n    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions\n    └── billing/\n        ├── CONTEXT.md\n        └── docs/adr/\n```\n\n## Use the glossary's vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `CONTEXT.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.\n\nIf the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal: either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/domain-modeling`).\n\n## Flag ADR conflicts\n\nIf your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:\n\n> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders), but worth reopening because…_\n# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title \"...\" --body \"...\"`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.\n- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --comments`, filtering comments by `jq` and also fetching labels.\n- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"058e1846-e680-48d7-aaa1-0b5781bd35ff","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":1},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"msgrespin/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"msgrespin/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"msgrespin/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"wsl"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"85bd88f2-ae69-4e29-b301-76344ecc8bc1","session_id":"0bec5f8c-6b9c-4a75-b2f2-851783fcd0ab","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/msgre/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"d55082a0-c970-4dbc-bf2f-a5b6209d16dd","version":"2.1.286","gitBranch":"main"}
